is a family of functions
W_{g}:G\to G
for each
g\in G
which preserves the identity in each coordinate (i.e.
W_{e}(g)=W_{g}(e)=g
for any
g\in G
) and where
W_{hg}=W_{g}\circ W_{h}
together with term structure
W_{g}:T(h)\to T(W_{g}(h))
for any
g,h\in G
.

If each
W_{g}:G\to G
is a group homomorphism, this structure is a weakening system when the following diagrams commute
for every
g,h,k\in G
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{W_{h}(g)}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{g}k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{gh}k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{h}k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{W_{h}(g)}}

Now consider the more particular example discussed in
4.27
, where
G
is still an arbitrary group, but
T(g)=\mathrm{Aut}(G)
for all
g\in G
. We can let each
W_{g}:G\to G
be
\varphi_{g}
, the conjugation automorphism sending
h
to
ghg^{-1}
, and we can let each
W_{g}:T(k)\to T(k)
be ‘conjugation by conjugation’ taking each automorphism
x\in T(k)
to
\varphi_{g}x\varphi_{g}^{-1}
. Since
\varphi_{h}\varphi_{g}=\varphi_{\varphi_{h}(g)}\varphi_{h}

, the left-hand diagram above commutes, and using that equation we find that

\varphi_{h}\varphi_{g}(-)\varphi_{g}^{-1}\varphi_{h}^{-1}=\varphi_{\varphi_{h}(g)}\varphi_{h}(-)\varphi_{\varphi_{h}(g)}^{-1}\varphi_{h}^{-1}
so the right-hand diagram commutes.

4.2.3
Projection systems

Definition 4.36
.

A
pre-projection system
is a pre-weakening system
\mathcal{F}
equipped with an element

\mathsf{idtm}_{A}\in T(W_{A}(A))
for every
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
\Gamma\in\mathcal{F}
.

Definition 4.37
.

A
pre-projection homomorphism

F:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{D}
is a pre-weakening homomorphism for which

F(\mathsf{idtm}_{A})=\mathsf{idtm}_{F(A)}

for every
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
\Gamma\in\mathcal{F}
.

Definition 4.38
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a pre-projection system and
\Gamma
an object of
\mathcal{F}
.
The
slice pre-projection structure
on
\mathcal{F}/\Gamma

is given by the slice pre-weakening structure in
4.30

together with
\mathsf{idtm}_{P}^{\Gamma}\coloneqq\mathsf{idtm}_{P}
,
for every
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
and
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
.

Definition 4.39
.

A
projection system
is a pre-projection system for which every
W_{A}
is
a pre-projection homomorphism. A
projection homomorphism
is a pre-projection homomorphism
between projection systems.

Corollary 4.40
.

For any object
\Gamma
of a projection system
\mathcal{F}
, the strict slice category
\mathcal{F}/\Gamma

is a projection system.

Example 4.41
.

Consider the weakening system on
\mathcal{N}
from
4.34
.
We can equip it with a projection structure defining,
for every
(n,k)
in
(\mathbb{N},\geq)
,
the element
\mathsf{idtm}_{n,k}\in T(W_{n,k}(n,k))=\mathbf{Set}([k],[n+k])

to be the final-segment inclusion

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[k]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n+k]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{k}^{n+k}}

The fact that each
W_{n,j}\colon\mathcal{N}/n\to\mathcal{N}/(n+j)
is a projection homomorphism is readily verified:

\displaystyle W_{n,j}(\mathsf{idtm}_{n+m,k})

\displaystyle=(i_{n}^{n+j}+\mathrm{id}_{m+k})i_{k}^{n+m+k}

\displaystyle=i_{k}^{n+m+j+k}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{idtm}_{W_{n,j}(n+m,k)}.

Example 4.42
.

Consider the particular example discussed in
4.35
where the underlying category is an arbitrary group
G
, each
T(g)
is the set of automorphisms of
G
, and
W_{g}
is conjugation by
G
both on elements of
G
and terms (automorphisms of
G
).

A pre-projection system consists of an element
\mathsf{idtm}_{g}\in\mathrm{Aut}(G)
for every
g\in G
. We will let
\mathsf{idtm}_{g}
be the identity automorphism on
G
.

For a projection system on a group
G
, we need
W_{g}(\mathsf{idtm}_{h})=\mathsf{idtm}_{W{g}(h)}
for every
g,h\in G
. In our particular example, this means
g1_{G}g^{-1}=1_{G}
, which holds.

4.2.4
The definition of E-systems

We can now give the definition of E-systems.

Definition 4.43
.

A
pre-E-system

\mathbb{E}
is a strict category
\mathcal{F}
with term structure
equipped with a chosen terminal object
[]
in
\mathcal{F}
, the structure of a pre-substitution system
S
,
the structure of a pre-weakening system
W
, and the structure of a pre-projection system
\mathsf{idtm}
.

Definition 4.44
.

A
pre-E-homomorphism
from
\mathbb{E}
to
\mathbb{D}
is a functor

H:\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{E}}\to\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{D}}
between the underlying categories
with term structure such that
F([]_{\mathbb{E}})=[]_{\mathbb{D}}
, which is a pre-substitution homomorphism, a pre-weakening homomorphism, and a pre-projection homomorphism.

Definition 4.45
.

An
E-system
is a pre-E-system
\mathbb{E}
such that

1.

each
S_{x}
is a pre-E-homomorphism,

2.

each
W_{A}
is a pre-E-homomorphism,

3.

S_{x}\circ W_{A}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{E}/\Gamma}
for any
x\in T(A)
and
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,

4.

S_{x}(\mathsf{idtm}_{A})=x
for any
x\in T(A)
and
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
, and

5.

S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}\circ W_{A}/A=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{E}/{\Gamma}.{A}}
for any
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
.

An
E-homomorphism

H:\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}

is a pre-E-homomorphism from an E-system
\mathbb{E}
to an E-system
\mathbb{D}
.
We write
\mathbf{Esys}
for the category of E-systems and E-homomorphisms.

Remark 4.46
.

The condition that each
W_{A}
is a substitution homomorphism asserts that
the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{{\Gamma}.{B}}.{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{W_{A}(B)}}.{W_{A}(Q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{W_{A}(B)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{A}/{B}.{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{W_{A}(y)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{A}/B}

of functors with term structure commutes for every

Q\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{B}
,
B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and each
y\in T(Q)
.

Likewise, the condition that each
S_{x}
is a weakening homomorphism
asserts that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{S_{x}(P)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}.{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{{\Gamma}.{S_{x}(P)}}.{S_{x}(Q)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}/P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{S_{x}(Q)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}/{P}.{Q}}

of functors with term structure commutes for every

Q\in\mathcal{F}/{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}
.

Corollary 4.47
.

For any object
\Gamma
of a E-system
\mathcal{F}
, the strict slice category
\mathcal{F}/\Gamma

is an E-system.

Example 4.48
.

We can finally show that the category with term structure
\mathcal{N}
from
4.19
can be equipped with the structure of an E-system.
It can be turned into a pre-E-system because of

4.26
,
4.34
and
4.41
.
Conditions
1
and
2
of
4.45
are left to the reader.
The other ones are verified as follows:

3
.

Given
f\colon[k]\to[n]
,
it is
S_{f}\circ W_{n,k}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{N}/n}

since
[\mathrm{id}_{n},f]i_{n}^{n+k}=\mathrm{id}_{n}
.

4
.

Given
f\colon[k]\to[n]
,
it is
S_{f}(\mathsf{idtm}_{n,k})=[\mathrm{id}_{n},f]i_{k}^{n,k}=f
.

5
.

Given
(n,k)
in
\mathcal{N}
,
it is
S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{n,k}}\circ W_{n,k}/(n,k)=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{N}/(n+k)}

since
[\mathrm{id}_{n+k},i_{k}^{n+k}](i_{n}^{n+k}+\mathrm{id}_{k})=\mathrm{id}_{n+k}
.

Example 4.49
.

Consider the situation in
4.42
where our underlying category is an arbitrary group
G
, the terms of each
g\in G
are
\mathrm{Aut}
, substitution
S_{x}:G\to G
is given by the automorphism
x
itself and substitution
S_{x}:T(h)\to T(S_{x}h)
, weakening
W_{g}:G\to G
, and weakening
W_{g}:T(h)\to T(W_{g}h)
are given by conjugation.

Understood as a category,
G
does not have a terminal object (unless it is trivial), but we can still understand the conditions of
4.45
. The condition
2
in that definition means that the following diagrams must commute for
g,h,k\in G
and
x\in T(h)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{W_{g}(x)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{g}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{g}k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(S_{x}k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(S_{W_{g}(x)}W_{g}k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{W_{g}(x)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{g}}

Since

\varphi_{g}x=\varphi_{g}x\varphi_{g}^{-1}\varphi_{g}
, the left-hand diagram above commutes, and since
\varphi_{g}x-x^{-1}\varphi_{g}^{-1}=(\varphi_{g}x\varphi_{g}^{-1})\varphi_{g}(-)\varphi_{g}^{-1}(\varphi_{g}x\varphi_{g}^{-1})^{-1}
, the right-hand square above commutes.

The condition
1
in
4.45
means that the following diagrams must commute for
g,h,k\in G
and
x\in T(h)
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{S_{x}(g)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(S_{x}k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{g}k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(S_{x}W_{g}k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{S_{x}(g)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}}

Since
x\varphi_{g}=\varphi_{x(g)}x
, the left-hand diagram commutes, and since
then
x\varphi_{g}-\varphi_{g}^{-1}x^{-1}=\varphi_{x(g)}x-x^{-1}\varphi_{x(g)}^{-1}
, the right-hand diagram commutes.

Condition
3
does not hold since (on
G
)
S_{x}\circ W_{g}=x\varphi_{g}
, and in general this is not the identity.

Condition
4
does not hold since
S_{x}\mathsf{idtm}_{g}=x1_{G}x^{-1}
which is not in general
x
.

Condition
5
does not hold since (on
G
)
S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{g}}W_{g}=1_{G}\varphi_{g}=\varphi_{g}
which is not the identity in general.

We introduce more convenient notation for weakening and substitution.

Definition 4.50
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
. Recall that
W_{A}:\mathcal{F}/\Gamma\to\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
acts on objects,
morphisms and terms. We introduce the infix form of weakening by
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
to be

\langle{A}\rangle{-}
. Thus, we will write

\displaystyle\langle{A}\rangle{B}

\displaystyle:=W_{A}(B)

for
B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma

\displaystyle\langle{A}\rangle{Q}

\displaystyle:=W_{A}(Q)

for
B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
Q\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{B}

\displaystyle\langle{A}\rangle{g}

\displaystyle:=W_{A}(g)

for
B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,
Q\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{B}
and
g\in T(Q)

Definition 4.51
.

Let
x\in T(A)
for a family
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
. The infix form of substitution
by
x
is taken to be
{-}[{x}]
. Thus, we will write

\displaystyle{P}[{x}]

\displaystyle:=S_{x}(P)

for
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}

\displaystyle{Q}[{x}]

\displaystyle:=S_{x}(Q)

for
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
and
Q\in\mathcal{F}/{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}

\displaystyle{g}[{x}]

\displaystyle:=S_{x}(g)

for
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
,
Q\in\mathcal{F}/{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}
and
g\in T(Q)

Definition 4.52
.

A (pre-)E-system is
stratified
if its underlying category is
stratified in the sense of
2.1

and the underlying functor of each
W_{A}
and
S_{x}
is stratified with respect to the stratification induced on slices.

A morphism of stratified (pre-)E-systems is
stratified
if its underlying functor is stratified.

The category of stratified E-systems and stratified E-homomorphisms between them is denoted by
\mathbf{\mathbf{Esys}_{s}}
.

Example 4.53
.

The E-system on
\mathcal{N}
from
4.48
is stratified
by the identity functor.

4.2.5
Pairing and the projections

The composition
{A}.{P}
of
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}

behaves like a strict
\Sigma
-type.
In this section we define the pairing term

\text{pair}^{A,P}:=\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}\in T(W_{P}(W_{A}({A}.{P})))

and the projections and prove several useful properties about them. The strictness
is found, among other things, in the fact that we can prove judgmental
\eta
-equality,
and that pairing is strictly associative.

In this section we make use of the infix form of the
weakening and substitution operations introduced in
4.50
and
4.51
.

Definition 4.54
.

Let
x\in T(A)
and
u\in T(S_{x}(P))
for
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
.
We define the
term extension of
x
and
u
to be

{x}.{u}:={{\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}}[{x}]}[{u}]\in T({A}.{P}).

It is well defined since

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{{A}.{P}}({A}.{P}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{{P}[{x}]}({A}.{P}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T({A}.{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{u}}

(11)

where
S_{x}\circ W_{{A}.{P}}=W_{{P}[{x}]}

because
S_{x}
is a weakening homomorphism
and
S_{x}\circ W_{A}=\mathrm{Id}
.

To prove anything about the term
{x}.{u}
, we need the following property.

Theorem 4.55
.

Let
x\in T(A)
and
u\in T(S_{x}(P))
for
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
.
Then we have

S_{{x}.{u}}=S_{u}\circ(S_{x}/P)\colon\mathbb{E}/{\Gamma}.{{A}.{P}}\to\mathbb{E}/\Gamma

Proof.

\displaystyle S_{{x}.{u}}

\displaystyle=S_{S_{u}(S_{x}(\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}))}

(By
4.54
)

\displaystyle=S_{S_{u}(S_{x}(\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}))}\circ(S_{u}\circ W_{S_{x}(P)})\circ(S_{x}\circ W_{A})

(By
4.45
.
3
)

\displaystyle=S_{u}\circ S_{S_{x}(\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}})}\circ W_{S_{x}(P)}\circ S_{x}\circ W_{A}

(By
4.45
.
1
)

\displaystyle=S_{u}\circ(S_{x}/P)\circ S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}}\circ W_{P}\circ W_{A}

(By
4.45
.
1
)

\displaystyle=S_{u}\circ(S_{x}/P)\circ S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}}\circ W_{{A}.{P}}

(By
4.28
.
2
)

\displaystyle=S_{u}\circ(S_{x}/P).

(By
4.45
.
5
)

∎

Corollary 4.56
.

For every
x\in T(A)
,
u\in T(S_{x}(P))
and
v\in T(S_{{x}.{u}}(Q))
we have

{({x}.{u})}.{v}={x}.{({u}.{v})}\in T({{A}.{P}}.{Q}).

Proof.

By
4.55
, we have

S_{v}\circ(S_{{x}.{u}}/Q)=S_{v}\circ(S_{u}/{Q}[{x}])\circ(S_{x}/{P}.{Q})=S_{{u}.{v}}\circ(S_{x}/{P}.{Q})
,
so associativity of term extension follows.
∎

Definition 4.57
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
. We define

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}

\displaystyle:=\langle{P}\rangle{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{idtm}_{P}

Lemma 4.58
.

Let
F\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}
be an E-homomorphism.
For every
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
,

x\in T(A)
and
u\in T(S_{x}(P))
, it is

F({x}.{u})={F(x)}.{F(u)},\qquad F(\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P})=\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{F(A),F(P)},\qquad\text{and}\qquad F(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P})=\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{F(A),F(P)}

Proof.

We compute:

\displaystyle F({x}.{u})=F({{\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}}[{x}]}[{u}])={{\mathsf{idtm}_{{FA}.{FP}}}[{Fx}]}[{Fu}]={Fx}.{Fu},

\displaystyle F(\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P})=F(\langle{P}\rangle{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}})=\langle{FP}\rangle{\mathsf{idtm}_{FA}}=\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{FA,FP},

\displaystyle F(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P})=F(\mathsf{idtm}_{P})=\mathsf{idtm}_{FP}=\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{FA,FP}

where the outer equalities hold by definition,
and the inner ones since
F
is an E-homomorphism.
∎

Lemma 4.59
.

For every
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
,

x\in T(A)
and
u\in T(S_{x}(P))
, it is

\displaystyle{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}[{{x}.{u}}]

\displaystyle=x,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}[{{x}.{u}}]

\displaystyle=u,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}.{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}.

Proof.

To show that
{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}[{{x}.{u}}]=x
, we use that

S_{{x}.{u}}=S_{u}\circ S_{x}/P
to show that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}[{{x}.{u}}]

\displaystyle={\left(\langle{P}\rangle{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}\right)}[{{x}.{u}}]

(By
4.57
)

\displaystyle={{\left(\langle{P}\rangle{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}\right)}[{x}]}[{u}]

(By
4.55
)

\displaystyle={\left(\langle{{P}[{x}]}\rangle{{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}[{x}]}\right)}[{u}]

(By
4.45
.
1
)

\displaystyle={\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}[{x}]

(By
4.45
.
3
)

\displaystyle=x

(By
4.45
.
4
)

To show that
{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}[{{x}.{u}}]=u
, note that

{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}[{{x}.{u}}]={{\mathsf{idtm}_{P}}[{x}]}[{u}]={\mathsf{idtm}_{{P}[{x}]}}[{u}]=u

Finally note that

{\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{A}}.{(W_{{A}.{P}}/A)(P)}=\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{{A}.{P}}

and
\mathsf{idtm}_{\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{{A}.{P}}}=(W_{{A}.{P}}/{A}.{P})(\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}})
.
Thus
{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}.{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}=\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}

follows from the commutativity of the outer square in the diagram below.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{{A}.{P}}}(\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{{A}.{P}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(W_{P}/P(\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{{A}.{P}}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{{A}.{P}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{{A}.{P}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{{A}.{P}}/{A}.{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{P}/P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

The bottom-right triangle commutes by
4.45
.
5
.
For the top-left one:

\displaystyle S_{\langle{P}\rangle{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}}\circ W_{{A}.{P}}/{A}.{P}

\displaystyle=S_{\langle{P}\rangle{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}}\circ W_{P}/(W_{A}({A}.{P}))\circ W_{A}/{A}.{P}

(By
4.28
.
2
)

\displaystyle=\left(W_{P}\circ S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}\circ W_{A}/A\right)/P

(By
4.45
.
2
)

\displaystyle=W_{P}/P.

(By
4.45
.
5
)

∎

Theorem 4.60
.

For every
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
,
the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\coprod_{x\in T(A)}T({P}[{x}])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T({A}.{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(x,u)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{x}.{u}}

is a bijection.

Proof.

The inverse
to the given map is defined
by
w\mapsto({\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}[{w}],{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}[{w}])
.
Thanks to
4.59
it is enough to show
that, for every
w\in T({A}.{P})
, one has

{{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}[{w}]}.{{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}[{w}]}=w.

4.58
gives us that
y

{{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}[{w}]}.{{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}[{w}]}={({\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}.{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}})}[{w}].

Thus the claim follows from

{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}.{\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}}=\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}
,
which holds again by
4.59
.
∎

One consequence of this theorem is that the set
T(\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma})
has exactly one element, see
5.18
.

4.3
Characterising B-systems as stratified E-systems

In this section we construct an equivalence of categories between B-systems and the subcategory of
\mathbf{Esys}
on the stratified E-systems and stratified homomorphisms.
The functor from B-systems to stratified E-systems is constructed in
Section

4.3.1
, the one in the other direction in
Section

4.3.2
.
That these form an equivalence is shown in
Section

4.3.3
.

4.3.1
From B-systems to stratified E-systems

Note first that
we obtain a functor
\mathbf{Bfr}\to\mathbf{Cat}
as the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Bfr}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{RtTr}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Grph}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Cat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}}

where
\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{F}
are the functors from
2.13

and
\mathbf{R}
is the forgetful functor from
4.6
.
Arrows in
\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B})
are of the form

(X,k)\colon(n+k,X)\to(n,\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X))
, for
X\in B_{n+k}
.

We begin by equipping
\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B})
with a term structure.
The B-frame
\mathbb{B}
already provides us with sets of terms for the edges of
\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B})
, namely
T(X,1):=\partial^{-1}(X)
.
In order to construct sets of terms for
(X,k)
for each
k
,
which we do in
4.62
,
we assume that
\mathbb{B}
comes with a substitution structure in the sense of
4.23
.
We then show in
4.65

that
\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}
gives rise to a functor
\mathbf{T}

from B-frames with substitution to strict categories with term structure.
Next, in
4.68
we provide
\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{B})
with a pre-E-system structure when
\mathbb{B}
is a B-system,
and prove in
4.69

that
\mathbf{T}
preserves and reflects weakening and projection homomorphisms.
Finally,
we show in
4.71
that
the functor
\mathbf{T}
lifts to a full and faithful functor from B-systems to stratified E-systems.

Problem 4.61
.

For every B-frame
\mathbb{B}
with substitution structure
S
,
to construct a term structure
T
on the strict category

\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{B}}:=\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B})
and to construct, for any
t\in T(X,k)
, a homomorphism of B-frames

S_{t}^{k}\colon\mathbb{B}/X\to\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)
.

Construction 4.62

(for
4.61
)
.

We define the term structure by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
More precisely, for any
X\in B_{n}
and
k\leq n
we will define a set

T(X,k)
and, for any
t\in T(X,k)
, a homomorphism of B-frames

S_{t}^{k}\colon\mathbb{B}/X\to\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)
.

For every
n
and
X\in B_{n}
, let

T(X,0):=\{*\}
and

S_{*}^{0}:=\mathrm{id}\colon\mathbb{B}/X\to\mathbb{B}/X
.

For every
n
and
X\in B_{n+1}
, let

T(X,1):=\partial^{-1}(X)\subseteq\tilde{B}_{n+1}

(12)

and
S_{x}^{1}\coloneqq S_{x}\colon\mathbb{B}/X\to\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}(X)

which is a homomorphism of B-frames by assumption.

Suppose now that, for every
m\leq n
and
Y\in B_{m}
, we have defined sets

T(Y,k)
for
k\leq m
and, for every
t\in T(Y,k)
,
a homomorphism of B-frames
S_{t}^{k}\colon\mathbb{B}/Y\to\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(Y)
.
Let
X\in B_{n+1}
and define, for
1\leq k\leq n
,

T(X,k+1):=\coprod_{t\in T(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)}T(S_{t}(X),1).

(13)

and, for
(t,x)\in T(X,k+1)
, a homomorphism of B-frames

S_{(t,x)}^{k+1}
as the composite below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k+1}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/S_{t}^{k}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{(t,x)}^{k+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{t}^{k}/X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}}

(14)

where
S_{x}
comes from the substitution structure
and
S_{t}^{k}
from the inductive hypothesis.
∎

Remark 4.63
.

1.

For every B-frame
\mathbb{B}
and
X\in B_{n}
,
there is an isomorphism of strict categories

\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B}/X)\cong\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B})/(n,X)

natural in
\mathbb{B}
which maps
(i,Y)
to
(n+i,Y)

and it is the identity on arrows.
It follows that, when
\mathbb{B}
is a B-system,
we can choose the identity as the action on the term structure.
Therefore this isomorphism of categories lifts to an
isomorphism of categories with term structure

(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B}/X),T_{\mathbb{B}})\cong(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B})/(n,X),T_{\mathbb{B}})
.

Once we establish an E-system structure on
\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B})
,
we will see that this isomorphism is in fact an isomorphism of E-systems.

2.

Let
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
be B-frames with substitution structure
and
H\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}
be a homomorphism of B-frames.
If
H
preserves the substitution structure,
then for every
X\in B_{n+k}
and
t\in T(X,k)
the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/H(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}H(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{t}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H/X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\tilde{H}(t)}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)}

(15)

commutes in
\mathbf{Bfr}
, where

\tilde{H}(t):=(\tilde{H}(t_{1}),\ldots,\tilde{H}(t_{k}))
.
Indeed, by definition of
S_{t}^{k}
in (
14
),
the square (
15
) factors vertically into
k
squares of the form in

4.11
.
1
,
each of which commutes if
H
preserves the substitution structure.

3.

Let
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
be B-frames with substitution structure
and
H\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}
be a homomorphism of B-frames.
Suppose that
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
have weakening structure and define,
for every
X\in B_{n+k}
, the homomorphism of B-frames

W_{X}^{k}\colon\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)\to\mathbb{B}/X
as the composite

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{X}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\mathsf{ft}^{k-1}(X)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{\mathsf{ft}(X)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{X}}

(16)

which we take to be
\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}}
if
n=k=0
.

If
H
preserves weakening structure,
then for every
X\in B_{n+k}
the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/H(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}H(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H/X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{X}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{H(X)}^{k}}

(17)

commutes in
\mathbf{Bfr}
.
Indeed, by definition of
W_{X}^{k}
in (
16
)
the square (
17
) factors vertically into
k
squares of the form in

4.11
.
2
,
each of which commutes if
H
preserves the weakening structure.

Problem 4.64
.

To lift the functor
\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}\colon\mathbf{Bfr}\to\mathbf{Cat}

to a functor
\mathbf{T}\colon\mathbf{SubBfr}\to\mathbf{TCat}

from the category
\mathbf{SubBfr}
of B-frames with substitution structure
and homomorphisms of B-frames that preserve the substitution structure,
to the category of strict categories with term structure.

Construction 4.65

(for
4.64
)
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
be B-frames with substitution structure.
For every homomorphism of B-frames
H\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}
,
the functor
\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(H)\colon\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{A}}\to\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{B}}
,
maps an object
(n,X)
to
(n,H(X))
and an arrow
(X,k)
to
(H(X),k)
.
Since
H(*)=*
, the functor
\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(H)
strictly preserves the (unique) terminal object.

To make
\mathbf{T}(H):=\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(H)
into a functor with term structure
note that, for every
t=(t_{1},\ldots,t_{k})\in T(X,k)
and
1\leq j\leq k
,
the function
\tilde{H}
restricts as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(\mathsf{ft}^{k-j}S_{t_{j-1}}\cdots S_{t_{1}}(X),1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{A}_{n-k+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T(\mathsf{ft}^{k-j}S_{\tilde{H}(t_{j-1})}\cdots S_{\tilde{H}(t_{1})}H(Y),1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{B}_{n-k+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{H}}

(18)

since
H
commutes with the functions
\mathsf{ft}
and
preserves the substitution structure in the sense of
4.11
.
It follows that

\mathbf{T}(H)(t):=(\tilde{H}(t_{1}),\ldots,\tilde{H}(t_{k}))\in T(\tilde{H}(X),k).

(19)

This makes
\mathbf{T}(H)\colon\mathcal{F}/(n,X)\to\mathcal{F}/(n-k,\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X))

into a functor with term structure.

The action of
H
on the sets
T(X,k)
is clearly functorial in
H
.
∎

Remark 4.66
.

1.

The functor
\mathbf{T}\colon\mathbf{SubBfr}\to\mathbf{TCat}

from
4.65
is faithful,
since the functors
\mathbf{R}\colon\mathbf{Bfr}\to\mathbf{RtTr}
,

\mathbf{G}\colon\mathbf{RtTr}\to\mathbf{Grph}

and
\mathbf{F}\colon\mathbf{Grph}\to\mathbf{Cat}
are faithful and
the sets
T(X,1)
for
X\in B_{n}
form a partition of
\tilde{B}_{n}
.

2.

For every B-frame with substitution structure
\mathbb{B}
,
it follows by
2.8
and
2.14
that
the underlying category of
\mathbf{T}(B)
is stratified by the functor
that maps
(X,k)\colon(n+k,X)\to(n,\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X))
to
n+k\geq n
.

Problem 4.67
.

For every B-system
\mathbb{B}
,
to construct a pre-E-system structure on the category with term structure

\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{B})
from
4.62
.

Construction 4.68

(for
4.67
)
.

4.62
provides a homomorphism of B-frames

S_{t}^{k}\colon\mathbb{B}/X\to\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)

for every
X\in B_{n+k}
and
t\in T(X,k)
.
The homomorphism
S_{t}^{k}
preserves the substitution structure
since it factors, as in
4.63
.
2
,
into
k
B-homomorphisms of the form
S_{x_{j}}
, where
x_{j}\in\tilde{B}_{n+k-j}
for
j<k
.

4.65
and
4.63
.
1

then yield a functor with term structure

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{B})/(n+k,X),T_{\mathbb{B}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{B})/(n,\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)),T_{\mathbb{B}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{t}:=\mathbf{T}(S_{t}^{k})}

(20)

as required.

For the pre-weakening structure,
consider the homomorphism of B-frames

W_{X}^{k}\colon\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)\to\mathbb{B}/X

defined in
4.63
.
3
.
Since
\mathbb{B}
is a B-system, each factor of
W_{X}^{k}
in (
16
)
is a homomorphism of B-systems and so is
W_{X}^{k}
.

4.65
and
4.63
.
1

provide a functor with term structure

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{B})/(n,\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{B})/(n,X).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{(X,k)}:=\mathbf{T}(W_{X}^{k})}

(21)

It remains to construct the pre-projection structure.
In fact, we will prove a little bit more.
We construct by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
for every
X\in B_{n}
and
k\leq n
, an element

\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k)}\in T(W_{(X,k)}(X,k))=T(W_{X}^{k}(X),k)

with the property that the triangle of B-homomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{B}/W_{X}^{k}(X)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{X}^{k}/X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k)}}^{k}}

commutes.
This additional condition is needed in the inductive construction.
For every
n
and
X\in B_{n}
, let

\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,0)}:=*\in T(W_{(X,0)}(X,0),0)=T(X,0).

For every
n
and
X\in B_{n+1}
, it is

\partial\circ\delta(X)=W_{X}(X)\in B_{n+2}
.
Thus we can define

\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,1)}:=\delta(X)\in T(W_{(X,1)}(X,1))=T(W_{X}(X),1)

(22)

and
S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,1)}}^{1}\circ W_{X}/X=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}/X}

by condition
5
in
4.12
.

Suppose now that we have defined,
for every
m\leq n
,
Y\in B_{m}
and
i\leq m
,
an element
\mathsf{idtm}_{(Y,i)}\in T(W_{(Y,i)}(Y,i))
such that

S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{(Y,i)}}^{i}\circ W_{Y}^{i}/Y=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{T}/(m,Y)}
.
Let
X\in B_{n+1}
. It follows from (
13
) that,
for every
1\leq k\leq n

T(W_{(X,k+1)}(X,k+1))=\coprod_{t\in T(W_{X}^{k+1}(\mathsf{ft}(X)),k)}T(S_{t}^{k}\circ W_{X}^{k+1}(X),1).

But
W_{X}^{k+1}=W_{X}\circ W_{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k}
,
thus

\bar{t}:=\tilde{W}_{X}(\mathsf{idtm}_{(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)})\in T(W_{X}^{k+1}(\mathsf{ft}(X)),k)

and

\begin{split}S_{\bar{t}}^{k}\circ W_{X}^{k+1}/\mathsf{ft}(X)&=S_{\bar{t}}^{k}\circ W_{X}/W_{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k}(\mathsf{ft}(X))\circ W_{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k}/\mathsf{ft}(X)\\
&=W_{X}\circ S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)}}^{k}\circ W_{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k}/\mathsf{ft}(X)\\
&=W_{X}\end{split}

(23)

by
4.63
.
2

and the fact that
W_{X}
preserves the substitution structure,
and assumption (
4.68
).
In particular,
T(S_{\bar{t}}\circ W_{X}^{k+1}(X),1)=T(W_{X}(X),1)

and we can define

\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k+1)}:=(\bar{t},\delta(X)).

(24)

It remains to check that

S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k+1)}}^{k+1}\circ W_{X}^{k+1}/X=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}/X}
.
This is indeed the case by (
14
),
(
23
) and condition
5
in
4.12
:

\displaystyle S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k+1)}}^{k+1}\circ W_{X}^{k+1}/X

\displaystyle=S_{\delta(X)}\circ\left(S_{\bar{t}}^{k}\circ W_{X}^{k+1}/\mathsf{ft}(X)\right)/X

\displaystyle=S_{\delta(X)}\circ W_{X}/X

\displaystyle=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}/X}.

This completes the construction of the pre-E-system structure.
∎

Lemma 4.69
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
be B-systems and
H\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}

a homomorphism of B-frames that preserves the substitution structure.

1.

The functor with term structure
\mathbf{T}(H)\colon\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{A}}\to\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{B}}

is a pre-substitution homomorphism.

2.

H
preserves the weakening structure if and only if

\mathbf{T}(H)
is a pre-weakening homomorphism.

3.

H
preserves the structure of generic elements if and only if

\mathbf{T}(H)
is a pre-projection homomorphism.

Proof.

1.

By definition of the pre-substitution structure in
4.68
and

4.63
.
1
,

\mathbf{T}(H)
is a pre-substitution homomorphism if
every image under
\mathbf{T}
of any square in
\mathbf{Bfr}
of the
form (
15
) commutes.
By
4.63
.
2
, such squares commute since

H
preserves the substitution structure.

2.

By definition of the pre-weakening structure (
16
) and

4.63
.
1
,

\mathbf{T}(H)
is a pre-weakening homomorphism if and only if
the image under
\mathbf{T}\colon\mathbf{Bsys}\to\mathbf{TCat}

of any square in
\mathbf{Bfr}
of the form (
17
) commutes.
By
4.63
.
3
, such squares commute if

H
preserves the weakening structure.
The converse holds since
\mathbf{T}
is faithful by
4.66
.
1
.

3.

By (
18
) and (
19
),

\mathbf{T}(H)
acts componentwise as
\tilde{H}
on a term
t\in T(X,t)
.
It follows that
\mathbf{T}(H)
preserves the terms

\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k+1)}=(\delta(\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)),\ldots,\delta(X))

for
X\in B_{n},k<n
if and only if

H
preserves generic elements.
∎

Lemma 4.70
.

For every B-system
\mathbb{B}
, the pre-E-system constructed
in
4.68
is a stratified E-system.

Proof.

First, we need to verify conditions
1
–
4

in
4.45
, as condition
5
holds by construction.

1
.

It follows from
4.69
and (
20
) since
S_{t}^{k}
,
as defined in (
14
), is a homomorphism of B-systems
when
\mathbb{B}
is a B-system.

2
.

As above, it follows by
4.69
and (
21
)
since
W_{X}^{k}
, as defined in (
16
),
is a homomorphism of B-systems.

3
.

The case
X\in B_{n},*\in T(X,0)
holds trivially.
The case
X\in B_{n+1},x\in T(X,1)
follows from
condition
3
in
4.12
and functoriality of
\mathbf{T}
.
The case
X\in B_{n+k+1},(t,x)\in T(X,k+1)
,
where
t\in T(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)
and
x\in T(S_{t}^{k}(X),1)
,
holds by induction and functoriality of
\mathbf{T}
as

\begin{split}S_{(t,x)}^{k}\circ W_{X}^{k+1}&=S_{x}\circ S_{t}^{k}/X\circ W_{X}\circ W_{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k}\\
&=S_{x}\circ W_{S_{t}(X)}\circ S_{t}^{k}\circ W_{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k}\\
&=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{B}/\mathsf{ft}^{k+1}(X)}\end{split}

by (
14
) and (
16
),
the fact that
S_{t}
is a pre-E-homomorphism,
and
4.12
.2 and the inductive hypothesis.

4
.

As above, the case
k=0
holds trivially and
the case
k=1
holds by condition
4
in
4.12
.
For
X\in B_{n+1},\ k\leq n
and
(t,x)\in T(X,k+1)
,

\begin{split}\tilde{S}_{(t,x)}^{k+1}(\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k+1)})&=\tilde{S}_{x}\circ\tilde{S}_{t}^{k}(\tilde{W}_{X}(\mathsf{idtm}_{(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)}),\delta(X))\\
&=\tilde{S}_{x}(\tilde{W}_{S_{t}^{k}(X)}\circ\tilde{S}_{t}^{k}(\mathsf{idtm}_{(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)}),\delta(S_{t}^{k}(X)))\\
&=(\tilde{S}_{x}\circ\tilde{W}_{S_{t}^{k}(X)}(t),\tilde{S}_{x}(\delta(S_{t}^{k}(X))))\\
&=(t,x)\end{split}

by (
14
) and (
24
),
the fact that
S_{t}
is a pre-E-homomorphism, the inductive hypothesis,
and conditions
3
and
4
in
4.12
.

Finally, the underlying category
\mathcal{F}=\mathbf{F}\mathbf{G}\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{B})
is stratified by
4.66
.
2
.
By definition, weakening and substitution functors preserve
the
\mathbb{N}
-component of objects and arrows.
It follows that
\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{B})
is a stratified E-system.
∎

Lemma 4.71
.

1.

The functor
\mathbf{T}\colon\mathbf{SubBfr}\to\mathbf{TCat}

from
4.65

lifts to a functor
\mathbf{B2E}\colon\mathbf{Bsys}\to\mathbf{\mathbf{Esys}_{s}}
.

2.

The functor
\mathbf{B2E}
is full and faithful.

Proof.

1
.

By
4.70
, it is enough to show that,
for every homomorphism of B-systems

H\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}
,
the functor with term structure

\mathbf{T}(H)\colon\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A})\to\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{B})

is a stratified homomorphism of E-systems.
By
4.69
,
\mathbf{T}(H)
is a homomorphism of E-systems.
It is stratified since it preserves the
\mathbb{N}
-component of objects and arrows
by definition.

2
.

The functor
\mathbf{B2E}
is faithful by
4.66
.
1
.
Let then
K\colon\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbb{A})\to\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbb{B})

be a stratified homomorphism of E-systems.
Since
K
is stratified,
the function on objects
K\colon\coprod_{m}A_{m}\to\coprod_{n}B_{n}

is the identity on indices and gives rise to a family of functions

H\colon\prod_{n}(A_{n}\to B_{n})
such that

K(n,X)=(n,H_{n}(X)).

(25)

This function commutes with
\mathsf{ft}
functions
since, for every
X\in A_{n+1}
, the arrow

K(X,1)\colon(n+1,H_{n+1}(X))\to(n,H_{n}(\mathsf{ft}(X)))
in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{B}}

is necessarily of the form
(n+1,Y)\to(n,\mathsf{ft}(Y))
.

The sets
T(X,1)
for
X\in A_{n+1}
form a partition of
\tilde{A}_{n+1}
,
thus the functions
K_{X}\colon T(X,1)\to T(H_{n+1}(X),1)
for
X\in A_{n+1}

glue together to form a function

\tilde{H}_{n+1}\colon\tilde{A}_{n+1}\to\tilde{B}_{n+1}

such that

\tilde{H}_{n+1}(x)=K_{\partial(x)}(x).

(26)

It follows that
\partial\circ\tilde{H}=H\circ\partial

since
\tilde{H}_{n+1}(x)\in T(H_{n+1}(\partial(x)),1)
.
Thus we have defined a homomorphism of B-frames
H\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}
.

Let
x\in\tilde{B}_{n+1}
.
Since
K
is a substitution homomorphism,
for every
Y\in B_{n+k+1}
such that
\mathsf{ft}^{k}(Y)=\partial(x)
, it is

\begin{split}(n+k,\tilde{H}_{n+k}\circ S_{x}(Y))&=K\circ S_{x}(n+k+1,Y)\\
&=S_{K_{\partial(x)}(x)}\circ K(n+k+1,Y)\\
&=(n+k,S_{\tilde{H}_{n+1}(x)}\circ H_{n+k+1}(Y))\end{split}

and, for every
y\in\tilde{B}_{n+k+1}
such that

\mathsf{ft}^{k}\circ\partial(y)=\partial(x)
, it is

\begin{split}\tilde{H}_{n+k}\circ\tilde{S}_{x}(y)&=K_{\partial\circ S_{x}(y)}\circ S_{x}(y)\\
&=S_{K_{\partial(x)}(x)}\circ K_{\partial(y)}(y)\\
&=\tilde{S}_{\tilde{H}_{n+1}(x)}\circ\tilde{H}_{n+k+1}(y).\end{split}

It follows that the homomorphism of B-frames
H
preserves the substitution structure.
Moreover, it is
\mathbf{T}(H)=K

by (
25
), (
26
)
and
4.65
.
Thus
\mathbf{B2E}(H)=\mathbf{T}(H)=K

once we show that
H
is a homomorphism of B-systems.

It remains to verify that
H
also preserve the weakening structure
and the structure of generic elements.
Since
K
is a projection homomorphism,
for every
X\in B_{n+1}
it is

\mathbf{T}(H/X\circ W_{X})=K/(n+1,X)\circ W_{(X,1)}=W_{K(X,1)}\circ K/(n,\mathsf{ft}(X))=\mathbf{T}(W_{H_{n+1}(X)}\circ H/\mathsf{ft}(X)).

The first claim then follows from faithfulness of
\mathbf{B2E}
.
Finally,
H
preserves generic elements

\tilde{H}_{n+2}\circ\delta(X)=K_{W_{X}(X)}(\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,1)})=\mathsf{idtm}_{K(X,1)}=\delta\circ H_{n+1}(X)

since
K
is a projection homomorphism.
∎

4.3.2
From stratified E-sytems to B-systems

We have constructed a full and faithful functor
\mathbf{Bsys}\to\mathbf{\mathbf{Esys}_{s}}
.
Here we construct a functor in the opposite direction.
We begin in
4.75

with a functor
\mathbf{E2B}
from stratified categories with term structures to B-frames.
In
4.77

we consider substitution, weakening and projection structures and
prove in
4.78
that

\mathbf{E2B}
maps homomorphisms into homomorphisms.
This allows us to lift
\mathbf{E2B}
to a functor
\mathbf{\mathbf{Esys}_{s}}\to\mathbf{Bsys}
in
4.80
.

Problem 4.72
.

Given a stratified category with term structure
(\mathcal{F},T)
,
to construct a B-frame
\mathbf{B2E}(\mathcal{F},T)
.

Construction 4.73

(for
4.72
)
.

For every object
X
in
\mathcal{F}
,
let
\overline{X}
denote the unique individual arrow with domain
X
.
For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define sets

\displaystyle B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n}

\displaystyle:=\left\{X\in\mathrm{Ob}{\left(\mathcal{F}\right)}\mid L(X)=n\right\}

(27)

\displaystyle\tilde{B}(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}

\displaystyle:=\coprod_{X\in B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}}T(\overline{X})

(28)

and functions
\mathsf{ft}_{n}\colon B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}\to B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n}

and
\partial_{n}\colon\tilde{B}(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}\to B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{ft}(X)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{cod}(\overline{X})

(29)

\displaystyle\partial(X,x)

\displaystyle:=X.

(30)

These definitions give rise to a B-frame
\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)
.
∎

Problem 4.74
.

To construct a functor
\mathbf{E2B}\colon\mathbf{TCat_{s}}\to\mathbf{Bfr}

from the category of stratified categories with term structure
and stratified functors with term structure
to the category of B-frames and homomorphisms.

Construction 4.75

(for
4.74
)
.

The action on objects is given by
4.73
.
Let then
F\colon(\mathcal{F},T)\to(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})
be a stratified functor with term structure.
We need to construct a homomorphism of B-frames

\mathbf{E2B}(F)\colon\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)\to\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})
.
Since
F
is stratified, it maps
B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n}
into
B(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})_{n}
.
For every
X\in B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}
, the functor
F
maps
the individual arrow
\overline{X}
to the individual arrow
\overline{F(X)}

by
2.10
.
It follows first that

\begin{split}F\circ\mathsf{ft}(X)&=F\circ\mathrm{cod}(\overline{X})\\
&=\mathrm{cod}(\overline{F(X)})\\
&=\mathsf{ft}\circ F(X),\end{split}

and secondly that
we can define, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, a function

\tilde{F}\colon\tilde{B}(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}\to\tilde{B}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})_{n+1}

such that
\partial\circ\tilde{F}(X,t)=F\circ\partial(X,t)
by

\tilde{F}(X,t):=(F(X),F(t)).

(31)

This defines a homomorphism of B-frames
\mathbf{E2B}(F):=(F,\tilde{F})
.
∎

Problem 4.76
.

Let
(\mathcal{F},T)
be a stratified category with term structure
and consider the B-frame
\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)
from
4.73

1.

From a stratified pre-substitution structure on
(\mathcal{F},T)
,
construct a substitution structure on
\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)
.

2.

From a stratified pre-weakening structure on
(\mathcal{F},T)
,
construct a weakening structure on
\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)
.

3.

From a pre-projection structure on
(\mathcal{F},T)
,
construct a structure of generic elements on
\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)
.

Construction 4.77

(for
4.76
)
.

1
.

For every
(X,t)\in\tilde{B}(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}
,
the functor with term structure

S_{t}\colon(\mathcal{F},T)/X\to(\mathcal{F},T)/\mathsf{ft}(X)
is stratified.

4.75
then yields a homomorphism of B-frames

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)/X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)/\mathsf{ft}(X).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{(X,t)}:=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{t})}

(32)

2
.

For every
X\in B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n}
,
the functor with term structure

W_{\overline{X}}\colon(\mathcal{F},T)/\mathsf{ft}(X)\to(\mathcal{F},T)/X
is stratified,
where
\overline{X}
denotes the unique individual arrow with domain
X
.

4.75
then yields a homomorphism of B-frames

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)/\mathsf{ft}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)/X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{X}:=\mathbf{E2B}(W_{\overline{X}})}

(33)

3
.

For every
X\in B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}
,
we can define

\delta(X):=(W_{X}(X),\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{X}})\in\tilde{B}(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+2}

(34)

since
\overline{W_{X}(X)}=W_{\overline{X}}(\overline{X})
.
∎

Lemma 4.78
.

Let
F\colon(\mathcal{F},T)\to(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})
be a stratified functor with term structure.

1.

If
(\mathcal{F},T)
and
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})
have stratified pre-substitution structure and

F
is a pre-substitution homomorphism,
then
\mathbf{E2B}(F)\colon\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)\to\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})

preserves the substitution structure.

2.

If
(\mathcal{F},T)
and
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})
have stratified pre-weakening structure and

F
is a pre-weakening homomorphism,
then
\mathbf{E2B}(F)\colon\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)\to\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})

preserves the weakening structure.

3.

If
(\mathcal{F},T)
and
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})
have stratified pre-projection structure and

F
is a pre-projection homomorphism,
then
\mathbf{E2B}(F)\colon\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)\to\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},T^{\prime})

preserves the structure of generic elements.

Proof.

1
.

We need to show that, for every
(X,t)\in\tilde{B}(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}
, it is

\mathbf{E2B}(F)/\mathsf{ft}(X)\circ S_{(X,t)}=S_{(F(X),F(t))}\circ\mathbf{E2B}(F)/X
.
This follows from (
32
), functoriality of
\mathbf{E2B}
and

F/\mathsf{ft}(X)\circ S_{t}=S_{F(t)}\circ F/X
,
which holds because
F
is a pre-substitution homomorphism.

2
.

We need to show that, for every
X\in B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n}
, it is

\mathbf{E2B}(F)/X\circ W_{X}=W_{F(X)}\circ\mathbf{E2B}(F)/\mathsf{ft}(X)
.
This follows from (
33
), functoriality of
\mathbf{E2B}
and

F/X\circ W_{\overline{X}}=W_{\overline{F(X)}}\circ F/\mathsf{ft}(X)
,
which holds because
F
is a pre-substitution homomorphism
and
\overline{F(X)}=F(\overline{X})
.

3
.

For every
X\in B(\mathcal{F},T)_{n+1}
, it is

\mathbf{E2B}(F)\circ\delta(X)=(F(W_{X}(X)),F(\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{X}}))=(W_{F(X)}(F(X)),\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{F(X)}})=\delta\circ\mathbf{E2B}(F)(X)

where the first and last equality hold
by (
31
) and (
34
),
and the middle one because
F
is a pre-projection homomorphism.
∎

Problem 4.79
.

To lift the functor
\mathbf{E2B}\colon\mathbf{TCat_{s}}\to\mathbf{Bfr}

to a functor
\mathbf{E2B}\colon\mathbf{\mathbf{Esys}_{s}}\to\mathbf{Bsys}
.

Construction 4.80

(for
4.79
)
.

Let
\mathbb{E}
be a stratified E-system.
Then
\mathbf{E2B}(\mathcal{F},T)
can be given the structure of a pre-B-system

\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{E})
by
4.77
.
To show that
\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{E})
is a B-system,
we need to verify conditions
1
–
5
of
4.12
.

1
,
2
.

Since every stratified functor with term structure of the form
S_{t}
,
W_{A}

is an E-homomorphism, it follows by
4.78

that the homomorphisms of B-frames constructed
in (
32
) and (
33
)
are homomorphisms of B-systems.

3
.

For
(X,t)\in\tilde{B}(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}
,
it is

S_{(X,t)}\circ W_{X}=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{t}\circ W_{\overline{X}})=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{E})/\mathsf{ft}(X)}

by (
32
), (
33
),
functoriality of
\mathbf{E2B}
and
4.45
.
3
.

4
.

For
(X,t)\in\tilde{B}(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}
,
it is

S_{(X,t)}\circ\delta(X)=((S_{(X,t)}\circ W_{X})(X),S_{t}(\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{X}}))=(X,t)

by (
32
), (
34
),
condition
3
just proved and
4.45
.
4
.

5
.

For every
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}
, it is

S_{\delta(X)}\circ W_{X}/X=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{X}}}\circ W_{\overline{X}}/X)=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{E})/X}

by (
32
–
34
),
functoriality of
\mathbf{E2B}
and
4.45
.
5
.

Finally, for every stratified E-homomorphism
F\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}
,
the homomorphism of B-frames

\mathbf{E2B}(F)\colon\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{E})\to\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{D})

is a homomorphism of B-systems by
4.78
.
∎

4.3.3
Equivalence of B-systems and stratified E-systems

Here we show in
4.83
that
the functors
\mathbf{B2E}
from
4.71

and
\mathbf{E2B}
from
4.80
form an equivalence of categories.
We do so by showing in
4.82
that

\mathbf{E2B}\colon\mathbf{\mathbf{Esys}_{s}}\to\mathbf{Bsys}
is an
essential section of the full and faithful functor
\mathbf{B2E}
.

Problem 4.81
.

For every stratified E-system
\mathbb{E}
,
to construct an isomorphism
\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{E}))\cong\mathbb{E}

of stratified E-systems, natural in
\mathbb{E}
.

Construction 4.82

(for
4.81
)
.

In this construction we decorate the structures from
\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{E}))

with a hat, as in
\hat{\mathcal{F}}
.
Since
\mathcal{F}
is stratified,
the function mapping
(n,X)\in\coprod_{n}B(\mathbb{E})_{n}
to
X

extends to an isomorphism
\varphi
between
the underlying strict category
\hat{\mathcal{F}}
of
\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(\mathbb{E}))
,
constructed in
4.62
, and
\mathcal{F}
.
In particular, it maps an arrow
(X,k)

to the arrow
\overline{X}^{k}:=\overline{\mathsf{ft}^{k-1}(X)}\circ\cdots\circ\overline{X}\colon X\to\mathsf{ft}^{k}(X)
in
\mathcal{F}
as in (
3
).

In order to lift
\varphi
to an isomorphism of categories with term structure,
we need to show that
\hat{T}(X,k)\cong T(\overline{X}^{k})

for every
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n}
and
k\leq n
,
where
\hat{T}(X,k)
is the set defined in
4.62
.
For every
X\in B_{n+1}
, by (
12
) it is

\hat{T}(X,1)=\partial^{-1}(X)=\left\{(Y,y)\in\tilde{B}(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}\mid Y=X,\,y\in T(\overline{Y})\right\}\cong T(\overline{X}).

Suppose that
\hat{T}(Y,j)\cong T(\overline{Y}^{j})

for every
m<n,Y\in B_{m}
and
j\leq m
.
It follows by (
13
) that

\hat{T}(X,k+1)=\coprod_{t\in T(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)}T(S_{t}(X),1)\cong\coprod_{t\in T(\overline{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k})}T(\overline{S_{t}(X)})\cong T(\overline{X}^{k+1})

where the last bijection follows from
4.60

since
\overline{X}^{k+1}=\overline{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k}\circ\overline{X}

and
\overline{S_{t}(X)}=S_{t}(\overline{X})
.
In other words, elements of
\hat{T}(X,k)
are lists of length
k

of pairs
(Y,y)\in\tilde{B}(\mathbb{E})_{n+j}
for
j=1,\dots,k
,
where
y\in T(\overline{Y})
,
and the action on terms of
\varphi
first acts componentwise
dropping the first component of each pair and then applies
the bijection from
4.60
.

Next, we show that this choice of isos is natural in
\mathbb{E}
.
Given a stratified E-homomorphism
F\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}
, we need to show that

\varphi_{\mathbb{D}}\circ\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))=F\circ\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}
.
The functor
\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))
maps an arrow
(X,k)
to
(F(X),k)
, thus

\varphi_{\mathbb{D}}\circ\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))(X,k)=\overline{F(X)}^{k}=F(\overline{X}^{k})=F\circ\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}(X,k).

since
F
preserves individual arrows by
2.10
.
The functor with term structure
\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))
maps

(X,x)\in\hat{T}(X,1)
to
(F(X),F(x))

by (
18
) and (
31
), thus

\varphi_{\mathbb{D}}\circ\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))(X,x)=F(x)=F\circ\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}(X,x).

Suppose now that, for every
m\leq n
,
Y\in B(\mathbb{E})_{m}
,
i\leq m

and
(Y,t)\in\hat{T}(Y,i)
, it is

\varphi_{\mathbb{D}}\circ\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))(Y,t)=F\circ\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}(Y,t)
.
Let
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}
and
(t,(X,x))\in\hat{T}(X,k+1)
, then

\begin{split}\varphi_{\mathbb{D}}\circ\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))(t,(X,x))&={{\left(\varphi_{\mathbb{D}}\circ\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))(t)\right)}}.{F(x)}\\
&={{\left(F\circ\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}(t)\right)}}.{F(x)}\\
&=F({\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}(t)}.{x})\\
&=F\circ\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}(t,(X,x))\end{split}

by definition of
\varphi
, inductive hypothesis,
4.58
,
and definition of
\varphi
again.
Therefore we conclude that, for every E-homomorphism
F\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}
,

\varphi_{\mathbb{D}}\circ\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(F))=F\circ\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}.

(35)

It remains to show that each component
\varphi_{\mathbb{E}}
is an E-homomorphism.

To show that
\varphi
is a weakening homomorphism,
note that for every
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+k}
, it is

\hat{W}_{(X,k)}=\mathbf{B2E}(W_{X}^{k})
by (
21
) and

\begin{split}W_{X}^{k}&=W_{\mathsf{ft}^{k-1}(X)}\circ\cdots W_{X}\\
&=\mathbf{E2B}(W_{\overline{\mathsf{ft}^{k-1}(X)}}\circ\cdots\circ W_{\overline{X}})\\
&=\mathbf{E2B}(W_{\varphi(X,k)})\end{split}

by, in order, (
16
); (
33
) and
functoriality of
\mathbf{E2B}
;
condition
4.28
.
1
in the case
k=0
and
condition
4.28
.
2
for
k>0
;
and definition of
\varphi
.
Moreover,
W_{\varphi(X,k)}
is an E-homomorphism, thus

\varphi
is a weakening homomorphism by (
35
).

To show that
\varphi
is a substitution homomorphism we reason by induction.
The case
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n}
and
*\in\hat{T}(X,0)
is trivial.
For every
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}
and
(X,x)\in\hat{T}(X,1)
, it is

\hat{S}_{(X,x)}=\mathbf{B2E}(S_{(X,x)})
by (
20
) and

S_{(X,x)}=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{x})=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{\varphi(X,x)})

by (
32
) and definition of
\varphi
.
Suppose now that, for every
m\leq n
,
Y\in B(\mathbb{E})_{m}
,
i\leq m

and
t\in\hat{T}(Y,i)
, it is
S_{t}=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{\varphi(t)})

as homomorphisms of B-systems.
Then for every
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}
,
k\leq n
and
(t,(X,x))\in\hat{T}(X,k+1)
,
it is
\hat{S}_{(t,(X,x))}=\mathbf{B2E}(S_{(t,(X,x))})
by (
20
) and

\begin{split}S_{(t,(X,x))}&=S_{(X,x)}\circ S_{t}/X\\
&=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{x}\circ S_{\varphi(t)}/X)\\
&=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{{\varphi(t)}.{x}})\\
&=\mathbf{E2B}(S_{\varphi(t,(X,x))})\end{split}

by, in order, (
14
);
inductive hypothesis, (
32
) and functoriality of
\mathbf{E2B}
;

4.55
; and definition of
\varphi
.
Therefore
S_{t}=\mathbf{B2E}(\mathbf{E2B}(S_{\varphi(t)}))

for every
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+k}
and
t\in\hat{T}(X,k))
.
We conclude that
\varphi
is a substitution homomorphism by naturality (
35
).

To show that
\varphi
is a projection homomorphism we reason by induction.
The case
(X,0)
for
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n}
is again trivial.
Let
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}
, then

\hat{\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,1)}}=\delta(X)=(W_{X}(X),\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{X}})

by (
22
) and (
34
).
Therefore
\varphi(\hat{\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,1)}})=\mathsf{idtm}_{\varphi(X,1)}

by definition of
\varphi
.
Suppose that, for every
m\leq n
,
Y\in B(\mathbb{E})_{m}
,
i\leq m
,
it is
\varphi(\hat{\mathsf{idtm}_{(Y,i)}})=\mathsf{idtm}_{\varphi(Y,i)}
.
Let
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+1}
and
k\leq n
. Then

\begin{split}\varphi(\hat{\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k+1)}})&=\varphi(W_{(X,1)}(\hat{\mathsf{idtm}_{(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)}}),\delta(X))\\
&={{\left(W_{\overline{X}}(\varphi(\hat{\mathsf{idtm}_{(\mathsf{ft}(X),k)}}))\right)}}.{\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{X}}}\\
&={{\left(W_{\overline{X}}(\mathsf{idtm}_{\varphi(\mathsf{ft}(X),k))}\right)}}.{\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{X}}}\\
&=\mathsf{idtm}_{\overline{\mathsf{ft}(X)}^{k}\circ\overline{X}}\\
&=\mathsf{idtm}_{\varphi(X,k+1)}\end{split}

by (
24
),
definition of
\varphi
and the fact that
\varphi
is a weakening homomorphism,
the inductive hypothesis,
5.20
,
and definition of
\varphi
again.
Therefore
\varphi(\hat{\mathsf{idtm}_{(X,k)}})=\mathsf{idtm}_{\varphi(X,k)}

for every
X\in B(\mathbb{E})_{n+k}
.
This concludes the proof that
\varphi
is an E-homomorphism.
∎

Finally we reach the main result of this section.

Theorem 4.83
.

The functors
\mathbf{B2E}\colon\mathbf{Bsys}\to\mathbf{\mathbf{Esys}_{s}}
from
4.71

and
\mathbf{E2B}\colon\mathbf{\mathbf{Esys}_{s}}\to\mathbf{Bsys}
from
4.80

form an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

As the functor
\mathbf{B2E}
is fully faithful by
4.71
.
2
,
it is enough to show that
\mathbf{E2B}
is an essential section of
\mathbf{B2E}
.
This holds by
4.82
.
∎

5
Equivalence between B- and C-systems

In this
Section
, we construct an equivalence between B-systems and C-systems,
in several steps.
We first construct an adjunction between the categories of CE-systems and of E-systems.
To this end, we construct, in
Section

5.1
, a functor from CE-systems to E-systems,
and, in
Section

5.2
, a functor in the other direction, from E-systems to CE-systems.
In
Section

5.3
we show that these functors form an adjunction that restricts to an equivalence when considering
rooted
CE-systems.
Finally, in
Section

5.4
, we give our equivalence between B-systems and C-systems, obtained by restricting the aforementioned equivalence to
stratified
rooted CE-systems and E-systems, respectively.

5.1
From CE-sytems to E-systems

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
be a CE-system.
For any
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
,
we define the
slice CE-system

\mathbb{A}/{\Gamma}
as follows.
Let
\mathcal{C}\left(\Gamma\right)
be the
strict category with the same objects as
\mathcal{F}/\Gamma

and with all arrows from
I(A)
to
I(B)
in
\mathcal{C}/\Gamma
as arrows from
A
to
B
.
The functor
I/\Gamma\colon\mathcal{F}/\Gamma\to\mathcal{C}/\Gamma

factors as an identity-on-objects
I_{\Gamma}
followed by a full and faithful one as shown in the diagram below.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}/\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\left(\Gamma\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I/\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\Gamma}}

We take
I_{\Gamma}
to be the underlying functor of
\mathbb{A}/\Gamma
.
The choice of pullback squares is induced by
\mathbb{A}
.

Remark 5.2
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
be a CE-system.

1.

For every object
\Gamma
, the identity
\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}

is terminal in
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{A}}}\left(\Gamma\right)
.
It follows that any slice CE-system is rooted.

2.

For every
f\colon\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
,
the functor
{f}^{*}\!{\,}\colon\mathcal{F}/\Gamma\to\mathcal{F}/\Delta
lifts to a functor

{f}^{*}\!{\,}\colon\mathcal{C}\left(\Gamma\right)\to\mathcal{C}\left(\Delta\right)
making the square below commute.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/\Delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\left(\Gamma\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\left(\Delta\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f}^{*}\!{\,}}

3.

For every
f\colon\Delta\to\Gamma

the commutative square in
5.2

lifts to a CE-homomorphism
{f}^{*}\!{\,}\colon\mathbb{A}/{\Gamma}\to\mathbb{A}/{\Delta}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
F\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}
be a CE-homomorphism.
Then for every
f\colon\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{A}}}

the diagram below commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbb{A}}}/\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbb{B}}}/F\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{A}}}\left(\Gamma\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{B}}}\left(F\Gamma\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbb{A}}}/\Delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbb{B}}}/F\Delta}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{A}}}\left(\Delta\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{B}}}\left(F_{\mathcal{C}}\Delta\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{F}}/\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{F\Gamma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(Ff\right)}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{C}}/\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\mathcal{F}}/\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I_{F\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F/\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(Ff\right)}^{*}\!{\,}}

Proof.

Commutativity of the back face follows from the fact that
{\left(Ff\right)}^{*}\!{\left(FA\right)}=F({f}^{*}\!{A})
for every
A\in\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbb{A}}}/\Gamma
,
commutativity of the front face follows from the universal property of pullbacks,
and commutativity of the other faces is immediate.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
be a CE-system. For every
f\colon\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}

and every
g\colon A\to B
in
\mathcal{C}\left(\Gamma\right)
the diagram below commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{\Delta}.{{f}^{*}\!{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\left({\Gamma}.{B}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\left({\Delta}.{{f}^{*}\!{B}}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}/{\Delta}.{{f}^{*}\!{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\left({\Gamma}.{A}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\left({\Delta}.{{f}^{*}\!{A}}\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I/{\Gamma}.{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I/{\Delta}.{{f}^{*}\!{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left({f}^{*}\!{g}\right)}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f}^{*}\!{\,}/B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I/{\Gamma}.{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I/{\Delta}.{{f}^{*}\!{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f}^{*}\!{\,}/A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left({f}^{*}\!{g}\right)}^{*}\!{\,}}

Proof.

This is
5.3
applied to
{f}^{*}\!{\,}

seen as a homomorphism of CE-systems thanks to
5.2
.
3
.
∎

Problem 5.5
.

To construct a functor
\mathbf{CE2E}\colon\mathbf{CEsys}\to\mathbf{Esys}
.

Construction 5.6

(for
5.5
)
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
be a CE-system with underlying functor
I\colon\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{C}
.
The underlying category of the E-system
\mathbf{CE2E}(\mathbb{A})
is
\mathcal{F}
.
The chosen terminal object is the one in
\mathbb{A}
.
To equip
\mathcal{F}
with a term structure we define,
for every
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
, the set

T(A):=\{x\colon\Gamma\to{\Gamma}.{A}\mid I(A)\circ x=\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}\}.

(36)

We define for any
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
, the functor

W_{A}:={A}^{*}\!{\,}\colon\mathcal{F}/\Gamma\to\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}.

(37)

Likewise, we define for any
x\in T(A)
, the functor

S_{x}:={x}^{*}\!{\,}\colon\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}\to\mathcal{F}/\Gamma.

(38)

These clearly extend to functors with term structure.
We also define
\mathsf{idtm}_{A}\colon T(W_{A}(A))
by the universal property of pullbacks
as in the diagram below.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\Gamma}{A}}.{W_{A}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}(I(A),A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I(A)}

As an immediate consequence of
5.4
, we get that
each functor
W_{A}
and
S_{x}
is both a weakening functor and a substitution functor.
It follows by the definitions that
weakening and substitution preserve the terms
\mathsf{idtm}_{A}
.

It remains to verify the remaining conditions of E-systems.

3
.

To show that substitution in weakened families is constant,
note that

S_{x}\circ W_{A}=x^{\ast}\circ A^{\ast}=(A\circ x)^{\ast}=(\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma})^{\ast}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}/\Gamma}.

5
.

The identity terms are neutral for pre-composition:

S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}\circ W_{A}/A=S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}\circ\pi_{2}(A,A)^{\ast}=(\pi_{2}(A,A)\circ\mathsf{idtm}_{A})^{\ast}=(\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}})^{\ast}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{F}}/{\Gamma}.{A}}.

4
.

The identity terms behave like identity functions: by the universal property,

S_{x}(\mathsf{idtm}_{A})
is the unique section of
A
such that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\Gamma}{A}}.{W_{A}(A)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}(\mathsf{idtm}_{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}(x,\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}(x,W_{A}(A))}

commutes. Thus, it suffices to show that this square also commutes with
x
in the place of

S_{x}(\mathsf{idtm}_{A})
. Note that
\pi_{2}(x,\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}})=x
.
Since
{{\Gamma}{A}}.{W_{A}(A)}
is itself a pullback, it suffices
and it is straightforward to verify the equalities

\displaystyle W_{A}(A)\circ\pi_{2}(x,W_{A}(A))\circ x

\displaystyle=W_{A}(A)\circ\mathsf{idtm}_{A}\circ x

\displaystyle\pi_{2}(A,A)\circ\pi_{2}(x,W_{A}(A))\circ x

\displaystyle=\pi_{2}(A,A)\circ\mathsf{idtm}_{A}\circ x.

Let now
F\colon\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{B}
be a CE-system homomorphism.
The underlying functor of
\mathbf{CE2E}(F)
is

F_{\mathcal{F}}\colon\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbb{A}}}\to\mathcal{F}_{{\mathbb{B}}}
,
which clearly preserves the choice of terminal objects,
while the action on terms is given by
F_{\mathcal{C}}
.
This functor with term structure is both a weakening and a substitution homomorphism
because of
5.3
.
Note that commutativity of the front square in the diagram in
5.3

is needed for the equations on the action on terms.
Finally, it is a projection homomorphism since it preserves identities.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

It follows immediately from the above construction that,
for every CE-system
\mathbb{A}
, the E-system
\mathbf{CE2E}(\mathbb{A})
has the property that

T(\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma})
is a singleton set for every
\Gamma\in\mathcal{F}
.
As we shall see in
5.18
, this is true for every E-system.
In fact, it will follow from
5.40
.
1
that
\mathbf{CE2E}

is essentially surjective on objects.

5.2
From E-systems to CE-systems

In this section we construct a functor from
\mathbf{Esys}
to
\mathbf{CEsys}
. We proceed in several steps: In
Section

5.2.1
we define the strict category of internal morphisms of an E-system. There are two kinds of morphisms in this category: internal morphisms from
A
to
B
in context
\Gamma
, and for any internal morphism
f:A\to B
in context
\Gamma
there are morphisms over
f
. There are also two kinds of composition, and in
Section

5.2.2
we prove an interchange law for them. In
Section

5.2.3
we complete the construction of the functor from
\mathbf{Esys}
to
\mathbf{CEsys}
.

5.2.1
The strict category of internal morphisms of an E-sytem

In this section we define for every E-system
\mathbb{E}
, and every context
\Gamma
in
\mathbb{E}
,
a category
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{E}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
. This goal is accomplished in
5.17
.
The empty context
[]
of
\mathbb{E}
, i.e. a terminal object in
\mathcal{F}
,
allows us to
have a non-trivial category structure on the contexts of
\mathbb{E}
.
In this case, the category structure is inherited from the category

\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{E}}:=\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{E}}\!\left([]\right)
.

Definition 5.8
.

For every
A,B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
we define the set

\mathrm{thom}(A,B):=T(\langle{A}\rangle{B}).

An element
f\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
is called an

internal morphism in context
\Gamma
.
We sometimes write
{f}\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
to indicate that
f
is
an internal morphism over
\Gamma
, or we may draw a diagram of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

but we shall generally omit the arrows down to
\Gamma
and say instead that we have
a diagram in context
\Gamma
.

Remark 5.9
.

Note that
\mathrm{thom}(\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma},A)=T(A)
for any
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,
because we have

W_{\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}}}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}}
.

Note also that
\mathrm{thom}({A}.{P},B)=\mathrm{thom}(P,\langle{A}\rangle{B})

for any
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
and
B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,
because
W_{{A}.{P}}=W_{P}\circ W_{A}
.
Once we have established a strict category of which the morphisms are given by

\mathrm{thom}(-,-)
, we therefore get that

{A}.{(-)}\dashv W_{A}.

The right adjoint to weakening by
A
, if it exists, will be the dependent
product
\Pi_{A}
.

Definition 5.10
.

Let
A,B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
.
For any
f\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
we define the
pre-composition
E-homomorphism

f^{\ast}:=S_{f}\circ W_{A}/B:\mathbb{E}/{\Gamma}.{B}\to\mathbb{E}/{\Gamma}.{A}.

We shall denote the action of
{f}^{*}\!{\,}
on a family
Q\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{B}
as
{Q}\circ{f}
.
Similarly, for every
C\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
, we shall write
{g}\circ{f}

for the action of
{f}^{*}\!{\,}
on
g\in\mathrm{thom}(B,C)=T(W_{B}(C))
.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
F\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}
be an E-homomorphism.
Then for every
{f}\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
in
\mathbb{E}
,
the square of E-homomorphisms below commutes.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}/{\Gamma}.{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{D}/{F\Gamma}.{FB}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}/{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{D}/{F\Gamma}.{FA}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F/{\Gamma}.{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{f}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(Ff\right)}^{*}\!{\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F/{\Gamma}.{A}}

Proof.

As
F
is both a weakening and a substitution homomorphism, it is

\displaystyle(F/{\Gamma}.{A})\circ{f}^{*}\!{\,}

\displaystyle=(F/{\Gamma}.{A})\circ S_{f}\circ(W_{A}/B)=S_{Ff}\circ(F/{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{W_{A}(B)})\circ(W_{A}/B)

\displaystyle=S_{Ff}\circ(W_{FA}/FB)\circ(F/{\Gamma}.{B})={\left(Ff\right)}^{*}\!{\,}\circ(F/{\Gamma}.{B}).\qed

Definition 5.12
.

Let
A,B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,
Q\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
and
R\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{B}
.
For every
f\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
we define

\mathrm{thom}_{f}(Q,R):=\mathrm{thom}(Q,{R}\circ{f}).

Remark 5.13
.

1.

The terms
\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}
from
4.57

are internal morphisms:

\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}\in\mathrm{thom}({A}.{P},A)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}\in\mathrm{thom}_{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}({A}.{P},P).

2.

Note that for
{g}\in\mathrm{thom}(B,C)
,
we have
g\circ f\in T(S_{f}(W_{A}/B(W_{B}(C))))
, whereas we would like that

g\circ f\in\mathrm{thom}(A,C)
. More generally, we can show that

S_{f}\circ(W_{A}/B)\circ W_{B}=W_{A}.

Since weakening is a weakening homomorphism, we have

S_{f}\circ(W_{A}/B)\circ W_{B}=S_{f}\circ W_{W_{A}(B)}\circ W_{A}.

By condition
3
in
4.45
we get that

S_{f}\circ W_{W_{A}(B)}\circ W_{A}=W_{A}.

Remark 5.14
.

The term structure of
{f}^{*}\!{\,}
, for
{f}\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
,
provides us also with an action on the internal morphisms over
B
.
To see how this extended notion of precomposition works, suppose we have a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

in context
\Gamma
, i.e.
{g}\in\mathrm{thom}(Q,R)
. Precomposing by
f
gives
us a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q}\circ{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R}\circ{f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{g}\circ{f}}

in context
\Gamma
.

Remark 5.15
.

Note that condition
5
in
4.45
asserts precisely
that
{(\mathsf{idtm}_{A})}^{\ast}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}}
for any
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
. In
particular, it follows that
g\circ\mathsf{idtm}_{A}=g
for any
g\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)

Lemma 5.16
.

For any
f\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
and
g\in\mathrm{thom}(B,C)
we have
f^{\ast}\circ g^{\ast}=(g\circ f)^{\ast}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle f^{\ast}\circ g^{\ast}

\displaystyle=S_{f}\circ(W_{A}/B)\circ S_{g}\circ(W_{B}/C)

\displaystyle=S_{f}\circ S_{W_{A}(g)}\circ(W_{A}/{B}.{W_{B}(C)})\circ(W_{B}/C)

\displaystyle=S_{S_{f}(W_{A}(g))}\circ(S_{f}/W_{A}(W_{B}(C)))\circ(W_{A}/{B}.{W_{B}(C)})\circ W_{B}/C

\displaystyle=S_{S_{f}(W_{A}(g))}\circ((S_{f}\circ(W_{A}/B)\circ W_{B})/C)

\displaystyle=S_{S_{f}(W_{A}(g))}\circ((S_{f}\circ W_{W_{A}(B)}\circ W_{A})/C)

\displaystyle=S_{S_{f}(W_{A}(g))}\circ W_{A}/C

\displaystyle=(g\circ f)^{\ast}.\qed

Theorem 5.17
.

1.

For every E-system
\mathbb{E}
and every object
\Gamma

in its underlying strict category
\mathcal{F}
,
objects of
\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and internal morphisms of
\mathbb{E}
over
\Gamma

form a strict category
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{E}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)
.

2.

Every E-homomorphism
F\colon\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}
induces a functor

F_{\Gamma}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{E}}\!\left(\Gamma\right)\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{D}}\!\left(F(\Gamma)\right)

for every
\Gamma
in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{E}}
.

Proof.

1
.

For
A,B\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,
the set of arrows from
A
to
B
is
\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
.
The fact that composition is associative is a direct corollary of

5.16
. The axiom
(\mathsf{idtm}_{A})^{\ast}=\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}}

implies that the identity morphisms satisfy the right identity law.
It remains to show that
\mathsf{idtm}_{B}\circ f=f
, which is a simple calculation:

\mathsf{idtm}_{B}\circ f=S_{f}\circ W_{A}(\mathsf{idtm}_{B})=S_{f}(\mathsf{idtm}_{W_{A}B})=f.

2
.

The action of
F_{\Gamma}
on arrows is given by the term structure of
F
.
Functoriality of
F_{\Gamma}
follows from
5.11
and the fact that
F
is a projection homomorphism.
∎

Now that we have a category structure,
we can state and prove the following consequence of
4.60
.

Corollary 5.18
.

1.

Let
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
Q\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{B}
,
then for every
f\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
there is a bijection

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi\colon T({Q}\circ{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left\{h\in\mathrm{thom}(A,{B}.{Q})\mid{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{B,Q}}\circ{h}=f\right\}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

given by
\varphi(t)={f}.{t}
.

2.

For every object
\Gamma
,

T(\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma})=\{\mathsf{idtm}_{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}}\}
.

Proof.

1
.

4.60
yields the following bijection:

\displaystyle\mathrm{thom}(A,{B}.{Q})

\displaystyle=T(\langle{A}\rangle{({B}.{Q})})

\displaystyle=T({\langle{A}\rangle{B}}.{\langle{A}\rangle{Q}})

\displaystyle\cong\coprod_{f\in T(\langle{A}\rangle{B})}T({\langle{A}\rangle{Q}}[{f}])

\displaystyle=\coprod_{f\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)}T({Q}\circ{f}).

Also, we find
{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{\langle{A}\rangle{B},\langle{A}\rangle{Q}}}[{h}]={\langle{A}\rangle{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{B,Q}}}[{h}]={\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{B,Q}}\circ{h}
.

2
.

The above bijection becomes in this case

T(\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}})\cong\{h\in\mathrm{thom}(A,A)\mid{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}}}}\circ{h}=\mathsf{idtm}_{A}\}=\{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}\}

where the second equality follows from

\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,\mathrm{id}_{{\Gamma}.{A}}}=\mathsf{idtm}_{A}
.
Since
\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}=W_{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}}(\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma})
,
the only element in
T(\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma})
is
\mathsf{idtm}_{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}}
.
∎

Theorem 5.19
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
and
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
.
Precomposition with
\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}
is weakening by
P
, i.e.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}/{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}/{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}\right)}^{\ast}=\,W_{P}}

Proof.

\displaystyle{\left(\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}\right)}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=S_{\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}}\circ W_{{A}.{P}}/A

\displaystyle=S_{\langle{P}\rangle{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}}\circ W_{P}/W_{A}(A)\circ W_{A}/A

\displaystyle=W_{P}\circ S_{\mathsf{idtm}_{A}}\circ W_{A}/A

\displaystyle=W_{P}\qed

We conclude this section
with a description of the projections and the pairing operation of an E-system
in the image of the functor
\mathbf{CE2E}
from
5.6

in terms of the underlying CE-system structure.

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
\mathbb{A}
be a CE-system and consider the E-system
\mathbb{E}:=\mathbf{CE2E}(\mathbb{A})
.
For every object
\Gamma
,
every
A\in\mathcal{F}/\Gamma
,
P\in\mathcal{F}/{\Gamma}.{A}
,
it is

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{id}_{{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}},P\rangle\in\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{A}}}({{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P},{{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}.{\langle{{A}.{P}}\rangle{A}})

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{A,P}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathrm{id}_{{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}},\mathrm{id}_{{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}\rangle\in\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{A}}}({{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P},{{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}.{\langle{P}\rangle{P}})

and, for every

x\in T(A)
and
u\in T(S_{x}P)
, it is

{x}.{u}=\pi_{2}\left(x,P\right)\circ u\in\mathcal{C}_{{\mathbb{A}}}(\Gamma,{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}).

Proof.

The first two claims follow immediately from
4.57

and the definitions in
5.6
.
The third claim follows from commutativity of the front-left face
in the diagram below.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma}.{S_{x}P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma}.{S_{x}P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Gamma}.{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\Gamma}.{A}}.{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{x}.{u}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}\left(x,P\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{x}P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}\left(x,P\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}.{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{S_{x}P}({A}.{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 8.19447pt\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{{A}.{P}}({A}.{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}\left(x,P\right)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}.{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W_{A}({A}.{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}.{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

This diagram commutes by definition, in the sense that
every square not involving the top row is a chosen pullback in
\mathbb{A}
,
and the remaining part commutes by definition of

\mathsf{idtm}_{{A}.{P}}
and
{x}.{u}
in
5.6
and
4.54
, respectively.
In this diagram we drop occurrences of the functor
I

and freely use notation from the E-system
\mathbf{CE2E}(\mathbb{A})
to increase readability.
∎

5.2.2
The interchange laws

We are now in the position to define vertical and horizontal composition, and
prove properties of them.
In particular, we conclude the section showing in
5.29

that every pair
{f}\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
and
F\in\mathrm{thom}_{f}(P,Q)
induces a morphism,

i.e.
a commuting square, from
\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{A,P}
to
\mathrm{pr}_{0}^{B,Q}
.

Definition 5.21
.

Let
{f}\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
and
F\in\mathrm{thom}_{f}(P,Q)
. Then we
define

{f\ltimes F:={\langle{P}\rangle{f}}.{F}}\in\mathrm{thom}({A}.{P},{B}.{Q})

Whenever we say that we have a diagram of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}

we mean that we have

f_{0}\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
,
f_{1}\in\mathrm{thom}_{f_{0}}(P,Q)
and

f_{2}\in\mathrm{thom}_{f_{0}\ltimes f_{1}}(R,S)
.

Lemma 5.22
.

Let
H:\mathbb{E}\to\mathbb{D}
be an E-homomorphism.
For every
{f}\in\mathrm{thom}(A,B)
and
F\in\mathrm{thom}_{f}(P,Q)
it is

H(f\ltimes F)=H(f)\ltimes H(F).

Proof.

H(f\ltimes F)=H({\langle{Q}\rangle{f}}.{F})={\langle{HQ}\rangle{Hf}}.{HF}=H(f)\ltimes H(F)
.
∎

Lemma 5.23
.

Vertical composition is associative.

Proof.

Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}

in context
\Gamma
.
Because weakening distributes over term extension, and term extension is
associative, we have

\displaystyle(f_{0}\ltimes f_{1})\ltimes f_{2}

\displaystyle={\langle{R}\rangle{({\langle{P}\rangle{f_{0}}}.{f_{1}})}}.{f_{2}}

\displaystyle={({\langle{R}\rangle{\langle{P}\rangle{f_{0}}}}.{\langle{R}\rangle{f_{1}}})}.{f_{2}}

\displaystyle={\langle{{P}.{R}}\rangle{f_{0}}}.{({\langle{R}\rangle{f_{1}}}.{f_{2}})}

(By
4.56
)

\displaystyle=f_{0}\ltimes(f_{1}\ltimes f_{2}).\qed
